Designing Technology with the Human Mind in Mind: Part 4

Rebecca Grier
Rebecca Grier

Follow
11 min read
·
Jan 23, 2024
109



Workload, Stress, & Total System Performance
Intro: This is the 4th in a series of articles to discuss what behavioural science knows about human cognition for those in product development. The 1st article explained why product makers should understand human cognition and introduced the dual system model of cognition (aka “Thinking Fast & Slow”). The 2nd article dove deeper into one part of that dual system model, “The filter.” The 3rd article introduced Working Memory. This article dives deeper into into cognitive workload & stress and how they impact performance. Future articles will discuss leadership, motivation, among other topics. All articles will explain why this knowledge is important to making technology that has a great user experience.
What is cognitive workload?
To understand cognitive workload one first must understand the dual system model of cognition, which I described in the first 3 articles. Briefly, all information that is sensed by the human sensory systems passes through the filter, which can choose to (a) act and bypass working memory, (b) “ignore” information, or (c) pass the information on to working memory. The information passed onto working memory can be individual sensations or it can be patterns. Working memory processes these sensations and patterns to create situation awareness, which feeds into decision making, which results in action (or not) being taken on the world. In working memory there are also the processes of sensemaking (creating new patterns) and metacognition (thinking about thinking).
Press enter or click to view image in full size
a cartoon detailing the dual factor model and that automation does not change what the human cognition system does.
All processing in working memory requires what are called attention resources. Cognitive workload is defined as the number of resources required by working memory to complete a task in relation to the number of resources available. It should be noted that attention resources are theoretical constructs. As of yet, we cannot point to something physiological and say that is an attention resource. Despite this lack of knowledge as to what resources are physiologically, we know that they exist and that they are limited.
One of the best-known examples of research on the limited resources within Working Memory is George Miller’s Magical Number 7+/- 2. Essentially, Miller found that we can hold 7 chunks of information in working memory. A chunk is a pattern that has been passed on from the filter. As described in the first article, the filter groups sensations into patterns and uses selective attention to determine what patterns or sensations are sent on to working memory. This is done to ensure that the amount of information to be processed does not exceed the number of resources available. Because if the task(s) do require more resources than are available, then we are in a task overload situation and our performance degrades. When our performance degrades. So, the filter does what it can to keep us out of an overload situation.
Expertise, Resources, & Cognitive Workload
It is important to remember that the filter passes along patterns and these patterns are unique to each human, the amount of resources needed to complete a task is unique to each person. In other words the number of resources required to complete a task varies from person to person. Therefore the cognitive workload experienced in any given task varies from person to person.
One of the most studied phenomena in psychology and decision making is the differences between experts and novices on cognitive tasks. Through this research, scientists that the key distinction between experts and novices is the patterns in long term memory . This research has shown that experts’ patterns typically are more complex than those of novices (Grier, 2015). That is experts’ patterns contain more information than novices’ patterns. This means that in experts the filter passes on more information or at least the information is organized in a way that requires less resources to obtain situation awareness, which is a key input to the decision making process.
As described in the second article in this series, situation awareness is most commonly defined as “the perception of environmental elements and events with respect to time or space, the comprehension of their meaning, and the projection of their future status” (Endsley, 1988). More succinctly there are three levels of Situation Awareness: (1) perception, (2) comprehension, and (3) projection. The more situation awareness a person has, the better quality of his/her decision making.
As experts have more complex patterns than novices, they need to spend less resources to develop situation awareness. So experts experience less workload. These more complex patterns typically result in better performance with less workload for experts. That is the expert does not need to use resources to create meaning and projection as the filter has recognized the pattern and passed it on as 1 chunk to Working Memory. Malcolm Gladwell described in “Blink: The Power of Thinking Without Thinking,” they may instantaneously and unconsciously make a decision.
However, when this occurs experts rarely can describe why they came to that decision. And in fact, asking an expert to describe how they know something can lead to worse performance as they try to reconstruct the patterns in their long term memory and begin to question their decision making ability. Novices are far more capable of explaining why or how they do something than doing it. Thus supporting the adage, “those who can — do; those who cannot — teach.”
Another disadvantage of the expert is that when the pattern is passed on, it may pass along information that is not there. I am certain that everyone has written text and not realized there was a typo. This is an example of the filter passing along a pattern and not what was actually sensed. It is also how magicians do their illusions. They take advantage of the universal patterns so that we sense what they want us to sense and not what is happening.
To be clear, the cognitive workload does not correlate to the demands of the task. The same task can lead to different levels of cognitive workload depending on the people who are performing the task and their expertise. Physiological factors such as intoxication, illness/injury, hunger, and fatigue can impact the workload experienced. All of these and other non-physiological factors can cause us to experience stress, which can also impact the number of resources available.
Cognitive Workload, Stress, & Performance
Recall that the definition of workload is the number of resources required by working memory to complete a task in relation to the number of resources available. Resources are physiological; so if we are intoxicated, ill, injured, hungry, or tired, we will have fewer resources available. So even if a task requires the same number of resources on Monday and Tuesday, if on Tuesday we are tired/ill/hungry, we will experience higher workload due to fewer resources being available.
In addition, the number of resources available can change based on the experience of stress. We have heard the stories of people doing extraordinary things in moments of extreme stress. This is because in those instances additional resources (attention and physical) are made available for the fight or flight response. However, these additional attentional resources are only available for short periods of time. After which the number of resources available drops precipitously. The precipitous drop allows our bodies a chance to recharge the pool of resources available. It should be noted this recharge is necessary. If we do not recharge after extreme stress or we experience stress for an extended period of time, there will be permanent changes to the structure of the brain.
Get Rebecca Grier’s stories in your inbox
Join Medium for free to get updates from this writer.

Subscribe
With the exception of the extreme situations, it is well documented that performance does not improve as stress increases. In 1908, the Yerkes-Dodson law established that there is an optimal level of stress where our performance increases. Part of this is due to the number of resources available, but part of it is due to task unrelated thoughts.
If the number of resources required by a task is far below the number of resources available, attention resources may be diverted away from the task to the base motivations associated with the 3 S’s (i.e., sleep, sustenance, and love). That is our mind wanders to one of these topics instead of focusing on the task at hand.
Press enter or click to view image in full size
If the number of resources needed exceeds resources available, we are in an overload situation. If the number of resources needed are fewer than available, resources are diverted to thoughts of fatigue, food, & fornication. This leaves a very small space where the resources required of a task results in optimal task performance
Performance degrades when there are too many and too few resources required by a task.
When we experience these low demand situations and our thoughts start to wander, one of two things can happen: vigilance decrement or complacency. The vigilance decrement occurs with high stress tasks and complacency occurs with low stress tasks.
The vigilance decrement is one of the most robust, but seemingly paradoxical findings in human factors. It was first studied in WII; when RADAR was invented a new position in aircraft was created where operators would monitor the RADAR displays for a clear signal that there was a German U-boat in the waters. They would then alert other entities, who would attack the U-boat.
It seems like a simple task; look at this display for a clear blip. In fact, detection was near 1005 during the first 15 min of watch. However, after 15min of watch, the chance of detection decreased precipitously. Furthermore, the operators would return to base as tired as the pilots.
To the casual observer it looks like the person is doing very little. However, 50 years of research has shown that the person is expending a lot of attention resources on preventing their mind from wandering or staying awake, because they understand the risks of not detecting every signal. So they are expending a great deal of resources to keep the task unrelated thoughts out of their working memory. This high level of resource usage leads to high cognitive workload. The vigilance decrement is the reason life guards and transportation security guards switch locations every 15min.
Press enter or click to view image in full size
When too many resources are required for a task, performance degrades because of cognitive overload. When a person is experiencing low stress in a task, performance degrades because of complacency. When there is high stress, then performance degrades due to the vigilance decrement (low demand task) or cognitive tunnelling. In extreme stress, we have additional resources for the fight or flight response that can lead to extraordinary performance, but at a cost.
Stress and resources required interact to impact performance.
Conversely, complacency is the failure to adequately monitor a situation (i.e., maintain vigilance) due to self-satisfaction with performance. In other words, if we believe that the task does not demand all of our attention, then we will allow unrelated thoughts to use resources in our working memory. Complacency has resulted in numerous accidents during “routine” medical transport calls, long duration space flight, war, among other situations. Though it is instinctual to think of complacency as a human failing it is indeed a normal and adaptive behaviour to a situation in which fewer resources are available. The filter is “aware” that fewer resources are available, so it sends fewer things on for processing. Complacency also commonly happens when a task has become routine.
Complacency occurs in low stress tasks with moderate and low resource requirements. The vigilance decrement occurs in high stress tasks with low demands. Cognitive tunnelling is a phenomenon that occurs in high stress tasks with moderate demands. First described in combat pilots, cognitive tunnelling is when an individual has trouble making more strategic decisions. That is they are very much focused on the here and now and not on the wider context. It seems that stress reduces how much information working memory can process. Examples of cognitive tunnelling exist outside of the military domain. For example, medical doctors have been documented to make poor decisions after being awake for numerous hours. It has also been seen in daily life, when people are exposed to longer term stressors such as poverty.
Thus stress and resources required have an interactive effect on an individuals performance. There is an optimal level of stress and resource requirements where an individual will have the highest performance. In future articles, I will return to this concept and describe how leadership and other organizational factors can impact individual performance.
Multiple Types of Resources
Thus far, I have described resources as if they are one type. That isn’t quite accurate. Chris Wickens posited in 1984 that there are multiple pools of resources and all evidence supports this assertion. Each pool of resources is available for different types of information at different stages of processing. The three stages of processing are (1) perception, (2) analysis, and (3) action. Information can come in via different sensory systems, which is one dimension of type. A second dimension of type is whether it is linguistic or graphical/tonal. Each combination of information type and stage of processing has a dedicated pool of finite resources.
So one can be in an overload situation and still have available resources. Those resources are just in another pool. Multitasking is completely possible when using multiple pools of resources. There are limits though. Each of the three stages of processing have a finite limit that cannot be exceeded and each dimension of type of information have a finite number of resources. So, we are able to do more multi-tasking when the tasks use very different resources than when there is overlap in the tasking.
Press enter or click to view image in full size

The careful reader will notice that the 3 stages of processing do not align perfectly with the stages of processing in working memory. Perceptual resources are dedicated to attaining the first level of situation awareness, perception. Action resources are dedicated to taking action. Analysis resources for everything else that occurs in working memory. This includes attaining the second and third levels of situation awareness (i.e., comprehension & projection), decision making, sensemaking, and metacognition. Thus the patterns that are used by the filter are even more critical to reducing workload. As the analysis resources are used for most of working memory.
Summary
In summary, all the cognition that occurs in working memory requires attention resources. Cognitive workload is the number of attention resources required for a task compared to the number of attention resources available. The number of attention resources available can change based on our health and our experience of stress. When we are doing a task that does not have a lot of stimulation, our working memory will allow our mind to wander to our base motivations. If the task is important, then we will expend more resources to prevent mind wandering. Thus tasks that appear easy can be just as challenging as tasks that appear overwhelming in terms of information to be processed. Furthermore, there are multiple pools of resources. When tasks are spread across these pools, we can do more than when relying on a single pool. So when adding automation, we must consider how it changes the cognitive processes. This is best done through a task analysis and function allocation.
The next article will dive deeper into how to utilize this knowledge of the dual system model, resources, workload, & stress in the design of systems. This will talk in more detail about task analysis and function allocation. Future articles will also discuss how the user interface helps with creation of patterns known as mental models and how that impacts trust with automation, which is critical to successful human-system integration. Another topic for future articles in about how leadership and teammates can aid each other in staying in the zone of optimal performance.

What is cognitive workload?
To understand cognitive workload one first must understand the dual system model of cognition, which I described in the first 3 articles. Briefly, all information that is sensed by the human sensory systems passes through the filter, which can choose to (a) act and bypass working memory, (b) “ignore” information, or (c) pass the information on to working memory. The information passed onto working memory can be individual sensations or it can be patterns. Working memory processes these sensations and patterns to create situation awareness, which feeds into decision making, which results in action (or not) being taken on the world. In working memory there are also the processes of sensemaking (creating new patterns) and metacognition (thinking about thinking).
Press enter or click to view image in full size
a cartoon detailing the dual factor model and that automation does not change what the human cognition system does.
All processing in working memory requires what are called attention resources. Cognitive workload is defined as the number of resources required by working memory to complete a task in relation to the number of resources available. It should be noted that attention resources are theoretical constructs. As of yet, we cannot point to something physiological and say that is an attention resource. Despite this lack of knowledge as to what resources are physiologically, we know that they exist and that they are limited.
One of the best-known examples of research on the limited resources within Working Memory is George Miller’s Magical Number 7+/- 2. Essentially, Miller found that we can hold 7 chunks of information in working memory. A chunk is a pattern that has been passed on from the filter. As described in the first article, the filter groups sensations into patterns and uses selective attention to determine what patterns or sensations are sent on to working memory. This is done to ensure that the amount of information to be processed does not exceed the number of resources available. Because if the task(s) do require more resources than are available, then we are in a task overload situation and our performance degrades. When our performance degrades. So, the filter does what it can to keep us out of an overload situation.
Expertise, Resources, & Cognitive Workload
It is important to remember that the filter passes along patterns and these patterns are unique to each human, the amount of resources needed to complete a task is unique to each person. In other words the number of resources required to complete a task varies from person to person. Therefore the cognitive workload experienced in any given task varies from person to person.
One of the most studied phenomena in psychology and decision making is the differences between experts and novices on cognitive tasks. Through this research, scientists that the key distinction between experts and novices is the patterns in long term memory . This research has shown that experts’ patterns typically are more complex than those of novices (Grier, 2015). That is experts’ patterns contain more information than novices’ patterns. This means that in experts the filter passes on more information or at least the information is organized in a way that requires less resources to obtain situation awareness, which is a key input to the decision making process.
As described in the second article in this series, situation awareness is most commonly defined as “the perception of environmental elements and events with respect to time or space, the comprehension of their meaning, and the projection of their future status” (Endsley, 1988). More succinctly there are three levels of Situation Awareness: (1) perception, (2) comprehension, and (3) projection. The more situation awareness a person has, the better quality of his/her decision making.
As experts have more complex patterns than novices, they need to spend less resources to develop situation awareness. So experts experience less workload. These more complex patterns typically result in better performance with less workload for experts. That is the expert does not need to use resources to create meaning and projection as the filter has recognized the pattern and passed it on as 1 chunk to Working Memory. Malcolm Gladwell described in “Blink: The Power of Thinking Without Thinking,” they may instantaneously and unconsciously make a decision.
However, when this occurs experts rarely can describe why they came to that decision. And in fact, asking an expert to describe how they know something can lead to worse performance as they try to reconstruct the patterns in their long term memory and begin to question their decision making ability. Novices are far more capable of explaining why or how they do something than doing it. Thus supporting the adage, “those who can — do; those who cannot — teach.”
Another disadvantage of the expert is that when the pattern is passed on, it may pass along information that is not there. I am certain that everyone has written text and not realized there was a typo. This is an example of the filter passing along a pattern and not what was actually sensed. It is also how magicians do their illusions. They take advantage of the universal patterns so that we sense what they want us to sense and not what is happening.
To be clear, the cognitive workload does not correlate to the demands of the task. The same task can lead to different levels of cognitive workload depending on the people who are performing the task and their expertise. Physiological factors such as intoxication, illness/injury, hunger, and fatigue can impact the workload experienced. All of these and other non-physiological factors can cause us to experience stress, which can also impact the number of resources available.
Cognitive Workload, Stress, & Performance
Recall that the definition of workload is the number of resources required by working memory to complete a task in relation to the number of resources available. Resources are physiological; so if we are intoxicated, ill, injured, hungry, or tired, we will have fewer resources available. So even if a task requires the same number of resources on Monday and Tuesday, if on Tuesday we are tired/ill/hungry, we will experience higher workload due to fewer resources being available.
In addition, the number of resources available can change based on the experience of stress. We have heard the stories of people doing extraordinary things in moments of extreme stress. This is because in those instances additional resources (attention and physical) are made available for the fight or flight response. However, these additional attentional resources are only available for short periods of time. After which the number of resources available drops precipitously. The precipitous drop allows our bodies a chance to recharge the pool of resources available. It should be noted this recharge is necessary. If we do not recharge after extreme stress or we experience stress for an extended period of time, there will be permanent changes to the structure of the brain.
Get Rebecca Grier’s stories in your inbox
Join Medium for free to get updates from this writer.

Subscribe
With the exception of the extreme situations, it is well documented that performance does not improve as stress increases. In 1908, the Yerkes-Dodson law established that there is an optimal level of stress where our performance increases. Part of this is due to the number of resources available, but part of it is due to task unrelated thoughts.
If the number of resources required by a task is far below the number of resources available, attention resources may be diverted away from the task to the base motivations associated with the 3 S’s (i.e., sleep, sustenance, and love). That is our mind wanders to one of these topics instead of focusing on the task at hand.
Press enter or click to view image in full size
If the number of resources needed exceeds resources available, we are in an overload situation. If the number of resources needed are fewer than available, resources are diverted to thoughts of fatigue, food, & fornication. This leaves a very small space where the resources required of a task results in optimal task performance
Performance degrades when there are too many and too few resources required by a task.
When we experience these low demand situations and our thoughts start to wander, one of two things can happen: vigilance decrement or complacency. The vigilance decrement occurs with high stress tasks and complacency occurs with low stress tasks.
The vigilance decrement is one of the most robust, but seemingly paradoxical findings in human factors. It was first studied in WII; when RADAR was invented a new position in aircraft was created where operators would monitor the RADAR displays for a clear signal that there was a German U-boat in the waters. They would then alert other entities, who would attack the U-boat.
It seems like a simple task; look at this display for a clear blip. In fact, detection was near 1005 during the first 15 min of watch. However, after 15min of watch, the chance of detection decreased precipitously. Furthermore, the operators would return to base as tired as the pilots.
To the casual observer it looks like the person is doing very little. However, 50 years of research has shown that the person is expending a lot of attention resources on preventing their mind from wandering or staying awake, because they understand the risks of not detecting every signal. So they are expending a great deal of resources to keep the task unrelated thoughts out of their working memory. This high level of resource usage leads to high cognitive workload. The vigilance decrement is the reason life guards and transportation security guards switch locations every 15min.
Press enter or click to view image in full size
When too many resources are required for a task, performance degrades because of cognitive overload. When a person is experiencing low stress in a task, performance degrades because of complacency. When there is high stress, then performance degrades due to the vigilance decrement (low demand task) or cognitive tunnelling. In extreme stress, we have additional resources for the fight or flight response that can lead to extraordinary performance, but at a cost.
Stress and resources required interact to impact performance.
Conversely, complacency is the failure to adequately monitor a situation (i.e., maintain vigilance) due to self-satisfaction with performance. In other words, if we believe that the task does not demand all of our attention, then we will allow unrelated thoughts to use resources in our working memory. Complacency has resulted in numerous accidents during “routine” medical transport calls, long duration space flight, war, among other situations. Though it is instinctual to think of complacency as a human failing it is indeed a normal and adaptive behaviour to a situation in which fewer resources are available. The filter is “aware” that fewer resources are available, so it sends fewer things on for processing. Complacency also commonly happens when a task has become routine.
Complacency occurs in low stress tasks with moderate and low resource requirements. The vigilance decrement occurs in high stress tasks with low demands. Cognitive tunnelling is a phenomenon that occurs in high stress tasks with moderate demands. First described in combat pilots, cognitive tunnelling is when an individual has trouble making more strategic decisions. That is they are very much focused on the here and now and not on the wider context. It seems that stress reduces how much information working memory can process. Examples of cognitive tunnelling exist outside of the military domain. For example, medical doctors have been documented to make poor decisions after being awake for numerous hours. It has also been seen in daily life, when people are exposed to longer term stressors such as poverty.
Thus stress and resources required have an interactive effect on an individuals performance. There is an optimal level of stress and resource requirements where an individual will have the highest performance. In future articles, I will return to this concept and describe how leadership and other organizational factors can impact individual performance.
Multiple Types of Resources
Thus far, I have described resources as if they are one type. That isn’t quite accurate. Chris Wickens posited in 1984 that there are multiple pools of resources and all evidence supports this assertion. Each pool of resources is available for different types of information at different stages of processing. The three stages of processing are (1) perception, (2) analysis, and (3) action. Information can come in via different sensory systems, which is one dimension of type. A second dimension of type is whether it is linguistic or graphical/tonal. Each combination of information type and stage of processing has a dedicated pool of finite resources.
So one can be in an overload situation and still have available resources. Those resources are just in another pool. Multitasking is completely possible when using multiple pools of resources. There are limits though. Each of the three stages of processing have a finite limit that cannot be exceeded and each dimension of type of information have a finite number of resources. So, we are able to do more multi-tasking when the tasks use very different resources than when there is overlap in the tasking.
Press enter or click to view image in full size

The careful reader will notice that the 3 stages of processing do not align perfectly with the stages of processing in working memory. Perceptual resources are dedicated to attaining the first level of situation awareness, perception. Action resources are dedicated to taking action. Analysis resources for everything else that occurs in working memory. This includes attaining the second and third levels of situation awareness (i.e., comprehension & projection), decision making, sensemaking, and metacognition. Thus the patterns that are used by the filter are even more critical to reducing workload. As the analysis resources are used for most of working memory.
Summary
In summary, all the cognition that occurs in working memory requires attention resources. Cognitive workload is the number of attention resources required for a task compared to the number of attention resources available. The number of attention resources available can change based on our health and our experience of stress. When we are doing a task that does not have a lot of stimulation, our working memory will allow our mind to wander to our base motivations. If the task is important, then we will expend more resources to prevent mind wandering. Thus tasks that appear easy can be just as challenging as tasks that appear overwhelming in terms of information to be processed. Furthermore, there are multiple pools of resources. When tasks are spread across these pools, we can do more than when relying on a single pool. So when adding automation, we must consider how it changes the cognitive processes. This is best done through a task analysis and function allocation.
The next article will dive deeper into how to utilize this knowledge of the dual system model, resources, workload, & stress in the design of systems. This will talk in more detail about task analysis and function allocation. Future articles will also discuss how the user interface helps with creation of patterns known as mental models and how that impacts trust with automation, which is critical to successful human-system integration. Another topic for future articles in about how leadership and teammates can aid each other in staying in the zone of optimal performance.

I wrote this article myself, write a linkedin post for him to share on linkedin his target audience is design strategists, cofounders, project managers, headhunters, recruiters, etc.

Why We Rejected the Super App Model for a Vertical Insurance Platform Redesign: Phase 1
Focus: Setting the stage, defining the problem, and the first critical decision (information architecture).
The Initial Mandate: Navigating the Strategic Crossroads:
Our project's first strategic win was validating the foundational information architecture (IA) through an immediate, targeted usability test. By decoupling architectural decisions from the aesthetic redesign, we proved that the Top-Bar navigation approach was the most efficient structure for managing a complex, multi-product ecosystem - directly supporting the corporate mandate to increase engagement and retention.
When tackling the redesign of a major mobile financial application, the first hurdle isn't visual - it's strategic. The architectural foundation determines every subsequent decision: navigation, content allocation, and long-term scalability.
Our journey began nine months ago with a clear goal - to transform a complex, legacy insurance app into a cohesive, engagement-driven platform. This is the story of how a single, high-stakes usability test validated our most crucial early decision.
I. The Challenge: A Burden of Complexity in a Reactive Sector
Strategic Shift: From Mass to PersonalizedBefore the redesign, the application reflected the typical struggles of mature financial products: high complexity and user friction. These were compounded by the inherently reactive nature of insurance - customers usually engage only after an adverse event (an accident, a claim, a health issue).
Prior to redesign - Allianz'ım AppOver time, the user experience had evolved organically, leading to fragmented information, inconsistent flows, and an outdated visual system. Key pain points were clear:
1. High Cognitive Load
Customers with multiple policies (Health, BES, Vehicle) found it difficult to locate information or complete routine tasks - especially in urgent situations. The use of a horizontal carousel to display policies forced users to swipe repeatedly to find details (e.g., from Health to Vehicle coverage). This interaction model increased cognitive load and wasted precious time, particularly in moments of crisis.
2. Limited Guidance & Cross-Sell
The application functioned mainly as a policy repository, not a financial guidance tool. The mid-section's "promiscuous" information architecture gave equal weight to self-service actions and external promotions, creating sensory overload. This lack of hierarchy obscured cross-sell opportunities and prevented the app from becoming an active engagement channel.
3. Architectural Failure & Dated UI
The Bottom Navigation Bar had reached its scalability limits, evidenced by the existence of a vague "Other" tab - an architectural catch-all for critical but non-core products. Combined with a dated UI, the experience failed to convey the professionalism and trustworthiness expected from a leading financial brand.
The Initial Mandate: Executing the Transformation
The strategic objective was to establish a modern, engagement-driven platform that would serve as the primary digital relationship channel between Allianz and its customers.
This vision aligned with a bold corporate mandate:
Transition from a traditional, agency-based model to a leading mobile insurance provider in Turkey within two years - achieving 50–60% mobile usage.
To reach this target, the platform needed to evolve through two key phases:
Phase 1: Operational Efficiency - Migrate agency functions to self-service (this phase was nearly complete).
Phase 2: Engagement & Retention - Improve usability, NPS, and satisfaction to drive retention and cross-sell.

The core transformation was therefore about shifting from documentation to activation and guidance - moving the app from a "Past/Today" model to a Future State that delivers personalized, value-driven solutions across both Lines of Business (LoB) and digital touchpoints.
Team & Timeline
The project officially launched in January 2025 as a collaboration between Ting (UX Design Partner), Product Management, and Development.
Operating in parallel tracks, our initial goal was to establish a functional navigation framework - allowing content strategy and component design to progress simultaneously.

🧠 Medium Article Master Prompt — “The 2.5-Year Lesson: Designing in a System That Wasn’t Ready To Be Designed”
Write a Medium article based on the following situation, structure, emotions, and insights:
Context
I worked 2.5 years as a product/UX designer for Allianz Türkiye through TING (Istanbul & London).
The product was a large-scale insurance ecosystem: apps, web, customer portals, omnichannel, VAS, new business cases, telesales, banking partnerships, and continuous new feature development.
I entered the job with passion, global standards, user-centric thinking, and a desire to create structured product systems.
But I walked into a reality vastly different from what I expected.
The Core Experience
Describe the following themes in depth:
1. Designing in a Low-Product-Maturity Culture
Many features came as “requests” without:
clearly defined problems
business logic
product brief
KPIs
user research
strategic alignment
Instead of designing screens, I spent most of my time:
clarifying business logic
reverse-engineering intentions
asking “why”
giving structure to ideas that had none
building reasoning that should have existed before
Insight:
“Most of my design time was not designing—it was cleaning up the thinking.”
2. When a Company Doesn’t Think in Systems
Each team worked feature-first, not product-first.
There was no central product spine governing:
rules
patterns
mental models
consistency
scalability
Every new feature became a mini war:
negotiating logic
fixing UX debts
preventing chaos from growing
Explain how this created:
constant rework
misalignment
UI and UX fragmentation
organizational fatigue
deep nervous system burnout
3. The Soul-Crushing Trade-off
To keep the product alive, I had to:
pour all my energy into business comprehension
define meaning where no meaning existed
articulate “the why,” “the how,” and “the what” on behalf of others
The result:
The time and energy that should have gone into crafting deeper UX, states, interactions, microcopy, and delight…
was sacrificed to make the project simply make sense.
This is the emotional center of the article.
4. Emotional Journey
Describe:
frustration
alienation
exhaustion
anger
helplessness
responsibility without authority
feeling like the only adult in the room
watching good design die before it even reached the screen
And also:
pride in the ability to hold structure in chaos
growth through hardship
learning product at a deeper level than most colleagues ever will
5. The Realization
Near the end of this period, I understood:
I wasn’t designing a product…
I was designing compensation for missing product maturity.
And the cost was:
my nervous system
my creative joy
my growth in craft
This is when the decision to leave became clear.
Transformation
Explain what changed:
After stepping back, I realized how much energy was wasted not designing, but compensating.
I saw the difference between:
companies that design products
companies that assemble features
I finally understood what a designer actually needs:
strategic clarity
product ownership
long-term thinking
system-level decisions
environments that respect design maturity
What I Learned (Key Takeaways)
Write 6–10 points such as:
Chaos teaches product thinking faster than courses ever will.
You cannot outwork a broken system.
One designer cannot carry the strategic responsibility of an entire organization.
Good design requires mature business thinking.
Design is not just pixels—it is organizational reasoning.
If the company can’t explain the “why,” the designer ends up doing someone else’s job.
What I Want From My Next Environment
State what a mature product environment looks like:
business comes prepared
PMs think clearly
product strategy exists before design starts
decisions scale across channels
designers can spend energy on:
craft
usability
interactions
research
deep refinement
Ending (Tone Options)
Choose one of the 3 styles:
1. Philosophical
“Some people learn design.
Others learn the conditions necessary for design to thrive.
I learned the second first.”
2. Empowering
“In 2.5 years, I became stronger than I ever wanted to be—because I learned to hold a product system together when nothing else did.”
3. Quietly Powerful
“Now I’m ready for an environment that is ready for me.”
How to Write the Article
Style guidelines:
Honest but not bitter
Insightful, professional, mature
No blaming of individuals
Focus on system-level patterns
Written as a lesson for designers and product teams everywhere
Tone:
thoughtful
stoic
experiential
strategic
reflective
educational
Optional Sections
You may add:
Screenshots or anonymized flows
Diagrams explaining the “chaos vs structure ladder”
Benchmarks from global best practices
A timeline of product maturity shifts
Personal nervous system journey (stress, growth, inner cost)
Purpose of the Article
Not to complain.
But to:
document the reality many designers never talk about
show the cost of low product maturity
help other designers see patterns faster
demonstrate leadership, maturity, and strategic understanding
build credibility with future employers and founders

--- [Message 211] Keywords: cto, po ---
Here is the article that I drafted before is there anything to get and merge with the one you written?

The Myth of B2B-fication: Why Consumer-Grade UX Fails Enterprise Complexity
For the better part of a decade, the rallying cry in enterprise software has been "Consumer-Grade UX." The premise is seductive: if we can make enterprise tools look and feel like Spotify or Airbnb, user adoption will skyrocket and training costs will vanish.
Stakeholders often come to us with a simple directive: "Make it intuitive. Make it look like the apps we use on the weekend."
But there is a fundamental flaw in this thinking. While "sleek" is desirable, the "B2C-fication" of B2B is largely a myth. It relies on a false equivalency that ignores the fundamental DNA of enterprise software. When we try to force complex financial systems, insurance back-offices, or legacy ERPs into the constrained simplicity of a social media feed, we don't just fail to solve the problem - we often break the product.
Here is why the "Consumer-Grade" approach fails when it hits the wall of Enterprise Complexity, and where the true balance lies.
The DNA Mismatch: Consumption vs. Orchestration
Consumer apps (B2C) thrive on constrained complexity. They excel at guiding a single user through a linear, high-frequency task: booking a ride, liking a photo, or ordering food. Their success lies in abstracting away the mechanics. You don't need to see the server logic to post a tweet.
Enterprise software (B2B), particularly in sectors like InsurTech or Fintech, exists in a different universe. It is built on:
Orchestrated Workflows, Not Isolated Tasks: A B2B user isn't just "filling out a form"; they are initiating a multi-stage underwriting process that triggers risk assessments, compliance checks, and cross-departmental approvals. The workflow is the product.
Data Density as a Feature: In B2C, whitespace is king. In B2B, information density is often a requirement. An underwriter assessing a risk profile doesn't want to click through ten screens of "airy" wizard steps; they need a high-density dashboard to correlate data points instantly.
The "Governance" Layer: Consumer apps assume the user owns their data. Enterprise apps must manage granular permissions (RBAC). A sleek interface that obscures who can see what isn't "clean design" - it's a security risk.

The Exception: Borrowing the Wrapper, Not the Engine
To be clear, this is not an argument for keeping enterprise software ugly or clunky. There is a "surface layer" where B2C principles are not only possible but necessary.
We can - and should - adopt consumer-grade standards for interaction patterns and feedback loops. This is where "B2C-fication" actually works:
System Feedback (Notifications): We can replace jarring, blocking browser alerts with non-blocking "Toast" notifications. This is a B2C pattern that improves the B2B experience without dumbing down the data.
Focused Context (Modals): Using clean, centered modal windows for isolated sub-tasks (like "Quick Add Policy" or "Edit Line Item") allows the user to perform an action without losing their place in the parent workflow.
Perceived Performance: Skeleton screens, smooth transitions, and optimistic UI updates are universal "delight" features that reduce cognitive load for everyone.

However, this is where the utility ends.
We can borrow the wrapper (the modal style, the notification animation), but we cannot borrow the engine.
Where B2C Logic Breaks the B2B Experience
The danger arises when we try to apply consumer Information Architecture to enterprise problems.
Imagine a Reinsurance Treaty Configuration screen. It involves 50+ inputs, conditional logic, and cross-referencing mathematical models.
The B2C Approach: Split this into a 20-step "Wizard" to make it feel simple.
The B2B Reality: You have just destroyed the power user's workflow. An expert user needs to see the inputs holistically to understand how changing Variable A affects Variable Z. By hiding the complexity behind "simple steps," you have forced them to click 20 times instead of scrolling once.

In enterprise design, clarity does not mean minimalism. It means navigable complexity.
The Product Designer's Role: Managing Complexity, Not Hiding It
As Product Designers, our job is so similar to architects; not to pretend the complexity doesn't exist. Our job is to build the Governance and Systems that make that complexity manageable.
We don't need to "Uber-ify" the insurance front-end. We need to build robust Design Systems that prioritize:
Scannability: clear hierarchies for massive datasets.
Traceability: clear audit trails for every decision made.
Efficiency: keyboard shortcuts, bulk actions, and density toggles that respect the professional's time.

The future of B2B design isn't about looking like a consumer app. It's about Enterprise-Grade Empathy: understanding that for a user processing 500 claims a day, a high-contrast, data-dense table is far more "delightful" than a beautiful, empty screen.

--- [Message 1] Keywords: cto, po, product owner ---
Agito Progress & Situation

Context: 
Murat is a 29-year-old Product Designer starting Day 1 at Agito, one of the largest insurance-tech software companies in Turkey. After months of struggling to land a product role in Europe, Agito became a strategic in-country opportunity to build real product ownership experience. He sees Agito as a 36-month to collect experience, credibility, behavioral patterns, and case studies that unlock a Europe/Netherlands move with €4–5K/month and eventually a long-term goal of **$450M net worth**. 

Current Situation: 

* He accepted. His first day is today.
* He had anxiety about receiving an **HP Windows laptop** after 6 years on Mac, worried it would slow him down; HR said they can discuss later.
* His mindset oscillates between high-performance ambition and “comparison kicks” when seeing doctors/lawyers/dentists.
* He knows his value: access, scalability, global mobility, digital leverage—not tied to Turkey’s salary ceiling long-term.
* He wants a powerful, controlled, strategic start at Agito.
* At 4 PM he has a welcome meeting with his manager.

**Psychology & Goals:**

* This is the **final Turkish step before takeoff**.
* His background: B2B insurance portals (Radity), B2C mobile app for Allianz Turkey (TING), and platform strategy work.
* Agito is the place to finally be **the only designer**, take ownership, build a system, influence tech, and produce undeniable high-value case studies.
* He wants elite psychological conditioning—Dan Peña tough-love, Jordan Peterson identity-building, and Tony Robbins expansion.
* He wants help navigating the first days, weeks, and months, including internal politics, expectations, building authority, and turning deliverables into portfolio gold.

**Where he needs support:**

1. **Day-by-day strategic guidance** (what to do, what to say, what to avoid).
2. **Scripts** for meetings, alignment, communication.
3. **Mindset calibration** to eliminate comparison & maintain internal alignment.
4. **Career architecture** to turn this role into a launchpad for Europe.
5. **Performance system** to dominate the first 90 days.
6. **Product design mastery in insurance domain** (UX heuristics, workflows, platform thinking).
7. **Daily accountability** on discipline, growth, reading, habit formation.

**Tone you should respond with:**

* Peña-level no-bullshit clarity
* Peterson-level identity & order
* Robbins-level strategic empowerment
* High-performance, elite, tactical
* Clear, concise, no soft talk

--- [Message 8] Keywords: po ---
Help me to do these 
Next 48 Hours:
1. Extract & map everything they showed you

Map the modules

Map the product areas

Map the POs

Map environments (lab/app)

Map rule pages

Map Make Files

Map segmentation work (Öykü Çakmak)

2. Build the “UX Readiness Gate — v0”

Simple, strong:
Bu 3 madde olmadan tasarıma başlamıyorum:

Kullanıcı: Kim? Problemi ne?

Akış: Mevcut-ideal süreç nedir?

Kural: İş kuralı nedir? Nerede değişir?

Put this into FigJam TODAY.
This becomes your authority.

3. Create your own FigJam workspace

Sections:

UX Ready Gate

Module flow maps

PO stakeholder lists

Quick win list

Benchmark inspirations

Before/after experiments

4. Go through the product templates

Evaluate:

What is missing?

What UX pieces must be added? (flows, personas, use cases)

5. Prepare a 1-page “UX Working Method for Agito”

This becomes your weapon in next meetings.

6. Review the app environment

List:

UI inconsistencies

Naming inconsistencies

Layout patterns

Component reuse opportunities

Confusing flows

Missing feedback/validation

Complex rule screens

This gives you your first quick wins.

--- [Message 32] Keywords: po, bileşen, poliçe, sistem ---
• Problem Alanları

Vade – poliçe – ödeyen üçlemesi karmaşık; kullanıcı zihninde “hangi parayı nereye dağıtıyorum?” sorusu net değil.

Çoklu poliçe dağıtımı (3 poliçe örneği) yeni → önceki sistem tek poliçe → kullanıcıların alışkanlığı kırılacak.

Kalan para senaryoları (otomatik dağıt, yeni vade, poliçe seçtir) henüz net bir kullanıcı akışıyla bağlanmamış.

Bilgi mimarisi “blog” mantığında; modüller arası ilişki kullanıcı tarafından anlaşılmayabilir.

Bazı iş kuralları hayat | emeklilik olarak farklı → kafa karıştırıcı Edge-case’ler çok.

• Karmaşıklık Noktaları

Hayat/Emeklilik farklı vadeler:

Hayat → ilk açık vadeye

Emeklilik → cari vadeden başla, geriye dağıt, sonra ileri
Bu iş mantığı UI içinde anlaşılır olmayabilir.

Şirket hesabı bazında takip + ödeyen bazında takip → 2 dünya birleşiyor.

“Poliçe eşleştirmeden bilgi kaydetme” isteği → Doğru bağlam yönetilmezse veri kaybı/hata riski.

• Kullanıcı Hatası Potansiyeli

Yanlış poliçeye dağıtım yapma

Yanlış vade seçimi

Kalan para senaryosunda yanlış seçenek seçme → yüksek finansal risk

Otomatik dağıtımın kurallarının tam anlaşılmaması

Çoklu poliçe ekranında yanlış işaretleme

• Navigasyon / Akış Varsayımları

Modül muhtemelen şöyle bir akışa sahip:
Para girişi → Vade seçimi → Dağıtım türü (manuel/otomatik) → Poliçe eşleştirme → Kalan para → Onaylama

“Wizard istemiyoruz” denmiş → UI içinde alt bileşenlerin dinamik olarak akıllanması bekleniyor.

“Dağıt” butonu konteks bazlı davranmalı:

Kalan para varsa

Yeni vade gerekiyorsa

Poliçe eşleştirilmediyse uyarı/popup

Ekran ekran ilerleme yerine tek ekranda modüler akış olmalı.

03.12.2025 Basım Eki

- Hakan ürün modülü, bes tarafıyla başlamayı planlıyor bir müşterimiz bes ürün tarafına odaklıyor


- Define X bize editör önermişti
- Her şirkette bu yöntem
- Kimisi
    - Yerleşimi
    - İçeriği
    - Güvenlik ve maskeleme

- Bu adamlara biz çeşitli yöntemlerle bunu sağlıyoruz.
- Kimi sistemde html e pdf dönüştürüyoruz tercihen postayla hard copy gönderilen işler.

- Word e benzer bir uygulama openoffice ile bilgileri replace edip pdf e çeviren bir yapı

Ama bunların hiçbiri olmadı.


- OpenOffice in kendi içinde bir zorlukları var.
- Kallavi bir server a ihtiyaç duyuyor. Bankalar üretim çıktısı olarak hemen poliçeyi bastırıp geri alıyor.
- Server ın gün içinde şişmemesi lazım.
- Yerleşim için ajanslara gidiyorlar.
- Dinamik alanlar için elle yazılan bir script dili kullandık.
- Bu da kimsenin hoşuna gitmiyordu.

- Drag drop ile.
- Template i kendi oluşturduğu sürükle bırak
- Her basımdan ücret almak istiyorlar, free bir şeyler bulduk 
- 

Özellikle dinamik alanlar için
- Ürünlerle bağlıcak, dinamik alanlar pdf e basıp convert etmek adamın

- İnput alan internet formu
- Pdf üretimi, basımı altyapısı
    - İnput girilecek formu değil, içerdeki değerlerle,

aslında ux framework ten kastetiğim şöyle bir şey bunu nasıl yazabiliriz?

🏛️ Enterprise UX Audit Framework: Utility Over Aesthetics
This framework translates the core tenets of the "B2C-ification Myth" debate into actionable criteria for evaluating an existing enterprise product. The primary focus of this audit is ensuring Enterprise-Grade Utility is delivered with Consumer-Grade Usability.

I. The Core Strategic Principle: Mission Precedence (Trust & Reliability)
The product's central mission—achieving data accuracy, speed, and reliability—must be prioritized over non-functional aspects like trendy aesthetics. The audit must confirm that the design fosters Trust and Credibility above all else.

Audit Criterion	Goal	Check for Failure/Risk
P-1: High-Stakes Transaction Trust	Does the design convey competence and stability necessary for business-critical operations?	Trust vs. Trendiness: Overuse of trendy, lightweight, or minimalist design elements that make the system feel unstable, like a "toy" rather than an "operational backbone."
P-2: Data Accuracy & Visibility	Are critical data and input fields instantly recognizable and clear?	Consequence Mismanagement: Ambiguous terminology, confusing data representations, or unclear system status that could lead to costly, high-consequence errors.
P-3: Stakeholder Alignment	Does the interface primarily serve the end-user's functional need (efficiency) or the buyer's aesthetic preference (visual delight)?	Split Stakeholder Problem: Excessive visual polish or marketing-focused elements that clutter the working interface and impede daily tasks.

Export to Sheets

II. Designing for the Expert User: Power, Precision, and Speed
Enterprise users operate in "Monotone Task Worlds" and prioritize efficiency. The audit must evaluate how effectively the system supports the Power-Efficiency Paradox by prioritizing the expert user's need for speed and control.

Audit Criterion	Goal	Check for Failure/Risk
E-1: Efficiency Tools & Shortcuts	Can expert users bypass standard flows for speed?	Power-Efficiency Paradox: Absence of keyboard shortcuts, batch actions, bulk editing features, or command-line interfaces for power users.
E-2: Scannability Over Minimalism	Can high-density, complex information be quickly processed?	Information Starvation: Excessive white space, large typography, or forced minimalism that pushes essential data off-screen, requiring unnecessary scrolling or navigation.
E-3: Precision Input Control	Are input mechanisms optimized for highly accurate and frequent data entry?	Friction: Use of large, clumsy touch targets or drop-down menus when a fast, type-ahead search or direct input would be faster and more precise.

Export to Sheets

III. Managing the Chaos of Utility: Reducing Cognitive Load
The audit must assess how the system balances high utility (necessary complexity) with low cognitive load (clarity and efficiency), reducing friction without eliminating necessary features.

Audit Criterion	Goal	Check for Failure/Risk
C-1: Progressive Disclosure (Information Management)	Is complexity revealed incrementally, reserving advanced options for when they are needed?	Overload: Presenting all functionality, settings, and advanced options on the primary screen, overwhelming the user. (E.g., lack of "Advanced Filters" or configurable views).
C-2: Smart Defaults & System Memory	Does the system proactively reduce the number of decisions the user must make?	Manual Burden: Absence of Smart Defaults (pre-filling fields based on historical use), System Suggestions, or maintaining user context across sessions (e.g., remembered filter states).
C-3: Real-Time Validation & Error Feedback	Is the system an effective co-pilot in high-stakes operations?	Punishment: Generic, delayed, or harsh error messages. Lack of Real-Time Validation (informing the user of an error before submission) to minimize rework and frustration.

Export to Sheets

IV. System-Carried Context: The Co-Pilot Experience
Enterprise software must act as a co-pilot, ensuring the user always knows where they are, what they are doing, and why it matters within their workflow.

Audit Criterion	Goal	Check for Failure/Risk
W-1: Role-Based Interfaces	Does the interface automatically adapt to the user's operational role and task?	Generic Views: A single, monolithic interface applied to all user roles, requiring unnecessary training or cognitive effort to filter irrelevant data/actions.
W-2: Guided Workflows	For non-expert or infrequent tasks, is the user guided step-by-step?	Abandonment: Long, multi-step processes (like account creation or complex reporting) that offer no visual progress indicator or clear path back to the previous step.
W-3: Navigational Predictability	Is the structure of the system consistent, stable, and transparent?	"Maze" Experience: Unpredictable or unstable primary navigation, hidden features, or inconsistency in interaction patterns across different screens.

Export to Sheets

This framework provides a clear checklist for an Enterprise UX Audit. Would you like me to elaborate on one of these sections, such as providing a deeper dive into the "E-1: Efficiency Tools & Shortcuts" criteria?

--- [Message 46] Keywords: ant design, yönetim, po, bileşen, figma, hasar, poliçe, sistem ---
Agito – Enterprise UX Operating System Stratejisi & İlk 90 Gün Aksiyon Planı (v2.2)

Tarih: 05.12.2025
Geliştirici: Single UX Designer

Amaç ve Kapsam

Agito için, modern B2C deneyimiyle beraber; operasyonel verimliliği ve geliştirme hızını uzun vadede artıran, hata oranını düşüren, hız ve modüler tutarlılık prensipleriyle çalışan bir Enterprise UX Operating System kurulması hedeflenmektedir.

İlk 90 Gün Taahhüdü: Bu sistemin ana Framework'ünü, somut Quick Win çözümlerini ve mevcut modüllerin değerlendirmelerini (hata/sürtünme noktaları) teslim edeceğim.

🎯 Kritik Hedefler ve İş Sonuçları (The ROI)

Temel amaç, tasarımdan kaynaklanan Technical Debt'i sıfırlamak ve geliştirme süreçlerine öngörülebilirlik getirmektir.

Kritik Hedef

Amaç

Beklenen İş Sonucu (İş Etkisi)

Ölçeklenebilirlik & Güvenilirlik

Final tasarımlarda Figma'dan koda tutarlı, component-based ve frame-based bir akış sağlama.

Developer Time (Geliştirici zamanı) ve QA süresinde öngörülebilir düşüş; tasarım dosyalarının "tek güvenilir referans" haline gelmesi.

Uzman Kullanıcı Verimliliği

Yoğun veri giriş/işlem ekranları için Expert User Efficiency prensiplerini uygulama (Örn: Kullanıcının doğal doldurma sırasına göre alanların yeniden düzenlenmesi)

Müşteri personelin işlem başına harcadığı süreyi kısaltma (Cost Reduction ve artan kapasite).

Sistem Tutarlılığı

Modüller arası Reusable Pattern yaklaşımını zorunlu kılma ve karmaşayı giderme.

Müşterilerin modüller arası kullanım/alışma süresini kısaltma, hata oranını düşürme. Tasarım/geliştirme ekipleri arasında iletişim yükünü azaltma (Ortak Dil).

1. Haftalık Kritik Tespitler

Mevcut tasarım üretim süreci, doğrudan Technical Debt'i tasarıma yansıtan kritik zayıflıklar göstermektedir:

Üretim Biçimi: Ant Design System'in görünümü taklit edilse de, üretim, component ve autolayout mantığından bağımsız, serbest çizim tabanlı ilerlemektedir. Bu durum, production-friendly olmayan, ölçeklenemez çıktılar yaratmakta ve tasarım dosyalarının güvenilirliğini ortadan kaldırmaktadır.

Pattern Eksikliği: Modüller arasında ortak pattern ve reusable logic eksiktir. Tasarımcılar, yeni bir bileşene ihtiyaç duyduklarında ya Ant Design'a ya da en yakın döneme ait dosyalara başvurmak zorundadır; bu da çizim zaman maliyetini sürekli artırmaktadır.

Odak Eksikliği: Benchmark analizi ve Enterprise-grade Usability prensipleri göz ardı edilmiştir.

2. Kurulacak Sistem – Enterprise UX İşletim Sistemi

Kurulacak UX Framework, temelinde B2B sigorta operasyonlarının gerektirdiği hız, doğruluk, tutarlılık ve güvenilirlik prensiplerini barındıracaktır.

Framework'ün 4 Ana Ayağı (Operasyonel Odak)

Ayak

Açıklama

İşlevsel Vurgu

Process Consistency & Data Validation

Veri giriş/işlem anında kritik bilgilerin componentlerinin tutarlılığını sağlamak, hata oranını minimuma indirmek ve Regülasyon Uyumu için gerekli işlem netliğini korumak.

Hata Önleme, Veri Kalitesi (Data Quality) ve Regülasyon Uyumu.

Expert User Efficiency

Kısayollar, batch operations (toplu işlemler), yüksek bilgi yoğunluğu yönetimi ve hızlı input mekanizmaları.

Operasyonel Verimlilik ve hız.

Cognitive Load Management

Progressive disclosure, smart defaults, real-time validation ile hata ve öğrenme eğrisini düşürme.

Kullanılabilirlik ve Hata Maliyeti azaltma.

System-Carried Context (Co-Pilot)

Rol bazlı ekranlar, guided workflows ve modüller arası navigasyon tutarlılığı.

Kullanım Kolaylığı ve akış bütünlüğü.

3. İlk 3 Ay Aksiyon Planı

A. Kısa Vadeli Kazanımlar (0–30 Gün)

Uçtan Uca Anlamlandırma: Devam eden kritik projelere dahil olup, end-to-end iş akışlarını anlama ve tasarımına müdahale etme.

Quick Win: Deneyim prensipleri ve Benchmark analizinden çıkan, geliştirilmesi Low-Effort (en az çaba) gerektiren ancak High-Impact (en yüksek etkiyi) yaratan friction noktalarının belirlenmesi, sunulması ve onay sonrası uygulanması.

Framework Temeli (v1): İlk UX Framework v1 dokümanının (kriterler, checklist, temel pattern library) oluşturulması.

Figma Temizliği: Component-based, production-based, documentation-based Figma yapısına geçiş için naming convention ve klasör yapısının belirlenmesi.

UX Governance: Kod review checklist'ine paralel çalışacak bir Design Review Checklist ve UX standartlarının belirlenmesi.

B. Orta Vadeli Kurumsallaşma (30–90 Gün)

Framework Genişletme (v2): Framework v2'nin ana modüllere (Örn: Poliçe, Hasar) göre genişletilmesi ve özelleştirilmesi.

Modül Değerlendirme Seti: Tüm kritik modüller için "Current State UX Evaluation" analizi ve somut öneri setlerinin hazırlanması.

Component Library Başlangıcı: Sadece UI değil, UX logic içeren (veri girişi, hata yönetimi vb.) Component Library'nin temelinin atılması.

Workflow Standardizasyonu: Guided Flow ve Role Logic ekseninde ana iş akışlarının standartlaştırılması.

Kurumsal Ritüel (Governance Gate): Ekiplerle ortak ritüel olarak 2 haftada bir "UX Review & Alignment" seansının başlatılması ve bu seansın, tasarımların koda geçiş öncesindeki kalite ve tutarlılık kapısı (Governance Gate) olarak konumlandırılması.

Şu şekil bir şey yazdım ama 3. Maddeden bir şey anlamadım

Agito – Enterprise UX Stratejisi & İlk 90 Gün Aksiyon Planı
Tarih: 05.12.2025
Hazırlayan: Murat Çetin
1. Amaç ve Stratejik Yaklaşım
Hedefimiz, sigorta operasyonlarının ihtiyaç duyduğu hız, tutarlılık ve ölçeklenebilirlik prensiplerine uygun, uzun vadede geliştirme maliyetini azaltacak bir tasarım çalışma sistemi kurmaktır.
Bu sistem, modern B2C kullanım kolaylığını korurken, kurumsal personelin beklediği güvenilirlik ve uzman kullanıcı ihtiyaçlarını önceliklendirecektir.
İlk 90 Gün Taahhüdü:
UX Framework’ün temel versiyonunu oluşturmak


Quick-win deneyim iyileştirmelerini teslim etmek


Mevcut modüllerin UX değerlendirmesini çıkararak somut öneriler sunmak

Hedefler
Amaç
Beklenen İş Sonucu
Tutarlılık & Ölçeklenebilirlik
Figma → Kod arasında component-based üretim
Geliştirici ve QA süresinde düşüş, tasarım dosyalarının tek güvenilir referans olması
Uzman Kullanıcı Verimliliği
Yoğun işlem ekranlarında hız odaklı düzenlemeler
İşlem başı zamanın kısalması,
Modüller Arası Tutarlılık
Modüller arası ortak pattern yaklaşımı
Hata oranında düşüş, ekipler arası iletişim yükünde azalma


3. İlk Haftalık Gözlemler (Kritik Tespitler)
İlk haftadaki incelemelerde, Figma üzerinde tasarım üretiminin modüller arasında farklılaştığı görülmüştür. Bu durum, component tutarlılığı, veri doğruluğu ve geliştirme aşamasında öngörülebilirliği azaltmakta ve tasarım dosyalarını güvenilir kaynak olmaktan çıkarmaktadır. Tasarım dosyalarında production uyumluluğu düşük, tekrar kullanılabilirlik limitli, domain-driven yaklaşım eksiktir.
Agito’nun modül çeşitliliği ve sigorta operasyonlarının karmaşıklığı göz önüne alındığında, tasarım üretim süreçlerinin aynı development gibi standardize edilmesi ve uzun vadede hem çizim zamanından kaynaklı üretim maliyetlerini azaltması kritik bir ihtiyaçtır.
Üretim Biçimi: "Ant Design System" dış görünüş olarak kullanılsa da, component ve autolayout mantığından bağımsız, serbest çizim tabanlı ilerliyor; bu da ölçeklenemez ve production-friendly olmayan çıktılar yaratmakla birlikte uzun vadede tasarım dosyalarını güvenilir bir kaynak olmaktan çıkarıyor.
Pattern Eksikliği: Modüller arasında ortak pattern ve reusable logic eksikliği, her yeni projede çizim zaman maliyetini arttırıyor.
Odak: Benchmark ve Enterprise-grade Usability prensipleri eksik.
4. Kurulacak Sistem: Enterprise UX Operating System
Bu yapı, Agito’nun bütün modüllerinde aynı tasarım dilini, aynı mantığı ve aynı kalite standartlarını zorunlu kılacak bir işletim sistemidir.
4 Ana Operasyonel Ayağı

Agito – Enterprise UX Operating System Stratejisi & İlk 90 Gün Aksiyon Planı (v3.1)
Tarih: 05.12.2025
Hazırlayan: Murat Çetin
1. Amaç ve Stratejik Yaklaşım
Hedefimiz, sigorta operasyonlarının ihtiyaç duyduğu hız, tutarlılık ve ölçeklenebilirlik prensiplerine uygun, uzun vadede geliştirme maliyetini ve tasarım kaynaklı maliyetleri(design debt) azaltacak bir tasarım çalışma sistemi kurmaktır.
Bu sistem, modern B2C kullanım kolaylığını korurken, kurumsal personelin beklediği güvenilirlik ve uzman kullanıcı ihtiyaçlarını önceliklendirecektir.
İlk 90 Gün Taahhüdü:
UX Framework’ün temel versiyonunu oluşturmak.
Quick-win deneyim iyileştirmelerini tespit etmek ve uygulamak.
Mevcut modüllerin UX değerlendirmesini çıkararak somut öneriler sunmak.
Hedef
Amaç
Beklenen İş Sonucu (İş Etkisi)
Tutarlılık & Ölçeklenebilirlik
Figma → Kod arasında component-based üretim altyapısı kurmak.
Geliştirici ve QA süresinde düşüş, tasarım dosyalarının tek güvenilir referans olması.
Uzman Kullanıcı Verimliliği
Yoğun işlem ekranlarında hız ve kullanım odaklı Expert User Efficiency düzenlemeleri yapmak.
İşlem başı zamanın kısalması (Örn: %30 form doldurma zaman tasarrufu), operasyonel maliyet düşüşü.
Modüller Arası Tutarlılık
Modüller arası ortak Reusable Pattern yaklaşımını zorunlu kılmak.
Yeni modülü öğrenme süresinin kısalması, üretim tasarım- geliştirme uyumluluğunun artıması ve ekipler arası iletişim yükünün azalması

2. İlk Hafta Gözlem Sonuçları
Mevcut tasarım üretim sürecinde gözlemlenen durumlar:
Üretim Biçimi: Ant Design System dış görünüş olarak kullanılsa da, component ve autolayout mantığından bağımsız, serbest çizim tabanlı ilerlemektedir. Bu durum, ölçeklenemez ve production-friendly olmayan çıktılar yaratmakla birlikte, tasarım dosyalarını güvenilir bir kaynak olmaktan çıkarmaktadır.
Pattern Eksikliği: Modüller arasında ortak pattern ve reusable logic eksikliği, her yeni projede çizim zaman maliyetini artırmaktadır.
Odak: Mevcut ekranlarda enterprise ürünlerde gerekli olan karmaşıklık yönetimi, iş öncelikli layout, akışların bu hususta sadeleştirilmesi ve modüller arası tutarlılık henüz sistematik olarak ele alınmamıştır. Bu nedenle interface seviyesinde dağınıklık, fazladan tıklama/aksiyon gereksinimleri ve domain’e özel iş senaryolarını desteklemeyen alanlar bulunmaktadır.
3. Kurulacak Sistem: Enterprise UX Operating System
Bu yapı, Agito’nun bütün modüllerinde aynı tasarım dilini, aynı mantığı ve aynı kalite standartlarını zorunlu kılacak bir işletim sistemi (Operating System) görevi görecektir.

Açıklama

Akış bütünlüğü ve kullanım kolaylığı.

Governance Başlangıcı: Kod review checklist’ine paralel çalışacak bir Design Review Checklist yazmak.
B) Orta Vadeli Kurumsallaşma (30–90 Gün)
Framework Genişletme (v2): Framework v1 çıktılarının onaylanması ve tasarım çalışmalarında projelerde uygulanmaya başlanması.
Component & Pattern Library (v1): Sadece Ant Design’dan alınan componentler değil, set halinde kullanılan modüllerin (tablo, menü, filtrelerin) UX logic içeren (veri girişi, hata yönetimi, filtre setleri) Pattern Library’sinin temelini atarak kod ekiplerine teslim etmek.
Workflow Standardizasyonu: Guided Flow ve Role Logic ekseninde ana iş akışlarının standardize edilmesi.
Governance Gate Kurulumu: Ekiplerle ortak ritüel olarak 2 haftada bir "UX Review & Alignment" seansının başlatılması ve bunun koda geçiş öncesi kalite kapısı (Governance Gate) olarak konumlandırılması.

Agito – Tasarım Planı (v3.1)
Tarih: 05.12.2025
Hazırlayan: Murat Çetin
Agito – Enterprise UX Operating System ve İlk 90 Gün Strateji Raporu
Hazırlayan: Murat Çetin — 05.12.2025

1. Giriş ve Stratejik Çerçeve
Agito’nun geliştirdiği modüller; yüksek hacimli işlem setlerini, yoğun veri girişlerini ve uzman kullanıcı rollerini barındıran enterprise-grade iş uygulamalarıdır. Bu bağlam, hem tasarım hem geliştirme tarafında:
yüksek tutarlılık,


tekrar kullanılabilir yapı,


risk azaltan akışlar,


düşük revizyon maliyeti,


uzman kullanıcı hızını artıran arayüzler


ihtiyacını doğurur.
Bu gereksinimler doğrultusunda amaç; Agito’nun tüm ürünlerine ortak bir tasarım ve üretim standardı getiren bir tasarım çalışma sistemi oluşturmaktır.
Bu yapı, tasarım kalitesini artırmanın ötesinde; geliştirme maliyetlerini düşüren, iş akışlarını hızlandıran, ürünlerin öğrenilme eğrisini kısaltan bir temel haline gelecektir.

2. Mevcut Durum: İlk Hafta Gözlemleri
2.1 Üretim Yaklaşımı Sorunları
Mevcut ekranlarda Ant Design temel alınsa da tasarım üretim tarafında:
Component yapısı korunsada ihtiyaçlar özelinde pattern üretimi yok.


Autolayout, frame gibi her bir ekranı ürünleştirecek özellikler kullanılamıyor


Tekrarlayan patternler, her bir ekran özelinde tutarlı olarak kullanılmamış. Manuel olarak çiziliyor.
Bu durum:
geliştirici maliyetini artırıyor,


revizyon döngülerini uzatıyor,


tasarım dosyalarını “tek doğru kaynak” olmaktan çıkarıyor.


2.2 Pattern Tutarsızlığı
Modüller arasında ortak davranış, layout, error-handling ve data-entry pattern’leri yok.
Sonuç:
Her modül “sıfırdan” tasarlanıyor


Öğrenme maliyeti artıyor


Tasarım yan etkileri operasyona yansıyor


2.3 Global Benchmark Eksikliği
Benzer B2B/Enterprise ürünlerle karşılaştırma yapılmadığı için:
Güncel örneklerden validasyon


UI/flow tasarımları sektör standardına hizalanmıyor




3. Tasarım Çalışma Sistemi
3.1 Design Production Layer
Figma dosya yapısı


Component & Pattern mimarisi


Component & Pattern lifecycle


Production-friendly tasarım üretimi


B. Interaction & Flow Layer
Yoğun form sayfalarının input türüne, doldurma önceliğine göre düzenlenmesi


Veri giriş hiyerarşisi


İş sırası temelli akış düzeni


C. Pattern Library
Reusable layout pattern’leri


İşlevsel pattern’ler (arama, filtre, tablo, detay, işlem)


Veri yoğun modüller için özel pattern seti


Error-handling ve boş durum rehberi


D. Quality Gate Layer
Design Review Checklist


Development-ready UI gereksinimleri


Pattern kullanım doğrulaması


UX risk kontrol listesi


Bu yapı tamamlandığında, Agito’nun her modülü ortak davranış, ortak görünüm ve tutarlı üretim mantığı taşıyacaktır.

4. Başarı Kriterleri (CTO Seviyesi)

Component kullanım oranında artış

Agito – Tasarım Planı
Tarih: 05.12.2025
Hazırlayan: Murat Çetin

1. Giriş ve Stratejik Çerçeve
Agito’nun geliştirdiği modüller; yüksek hacimli işlem setlerini, yoğun veri girişlerini ve uzman kullanıcı rollerini barındıran enterprise-grade iş uygulamalarıdır. Bu bağlam, hem tasarım hem geliştirme tarafında aşağıdaki kritik ihtiyaçları doğurur:
Yüksek tutarlılık ve tekrar kullanılabilir yapı (Scalability)
Risk azaltan akışlar ve düşük revizyon maliyeti
Uzman kullanıcı hızını artıran arayüzler (Efficiency)
Bu gereksinimler doğrultusunda amaç; Agito’nun tüm ürünlerine ortak bir tasarım ve üretim standardı getiren, modern B2B tasarım prensipleriyle uyumlu, geliştirme maliyetlerini düşüren ve iş akışlarını hızlandıran bir tasarım çalışma sistemi oluşturmaktır.
2. Mevcut Durum: İlk Hafta Gözlemleri 
2.1 Üretim Yaklaşımı
Ant Design sistemi görünüşte kullanılsa da tasarım sürecinde;
Frame ve autolayout yapıları aktif değil.
Ekranlar çoğunlukla manuel çiziliyor.
Tekrarlayan yapılar pattern olarak üretilmemiş.
Heri bir proje odağında ekranlar “tek doğru kaynak (source of truth)" olarak işlemiyor.
Bu durum hem geliştirme maliyetini artırıyor hem de revizyonları bütünüyle manuel hale getiriyor.
2.2 Benchmark Eksikliği
Mevcut tasarımların global enterprise ürünlerle kıyaslanmaması ve sektör standardına dair kaynak eksikliği; karmaşıklık, yoğun veri giriş akışları ve kurumsal kullanıcı için tasarım konularında büyük keşif fırsatlarına açık kapı bırakmaktadır.
3. Agito Unified Design Framework
Aşama
İçerik
Odak
A. Design Production Layer
Figma mimarisinin yeniden kurgulanması, Naming & documentation sistemi, Production-friendly dosya standartları.
Ölçeklenebilirlik ve tasarımın güvenilir dökümantasyon haline getirilmesi (single source of truth)
B. Interaction & Flow Layer
Veri girişindeki hiyerarşi ve önceliklendirme, Form yoğunluklu ekranların ve akışların analiz ve optimizasyonu.
Müşterinin ürün kullanım süresini kısaltma ve hata Önleme.
C. Design Pattern Library
Reusable layout setleri, Arama, filtre, tablo, detay, işlem pattern’leri ve Veri yoğun modüller için özel UI kalıpları.
Modüler Tutarlılık, tasarım sürelerinin kısalması, hand-off sürecinin hızlanması.
D. Quality Gate Layer
Design Review Checklist ve Üretim (handoff) kontrol noktaları (Governance).
Hand-off sürecindeki olası hataları azaltma
E. Framework & Guiding Principles Layer


Kurumsal ERP tasarımına özel modern prensiplerin, kuralların ve stratejik yaklaşımın (Agito Tasarım Framework v1) kılavuzunu oluşturmak
Tasarıma yönelik bir stratejik çekirdek belirleme.

4. Beklenen Çıktılar
Kriter Alanı
Kısa Vadede (0-90 Gün)
Orta Vadede (90+ Gün)
Geliştirme Maliyeti
Revizyon döngüsünde düşüş
Yeni modüller için tasarım süresinde kısalma. Tasarım borcunun (Design Debt)* azalması.
Kullanıcı Verimliliği
Layout kullanımlarının standardize edilmesi.
Modüller arası davranış tutarlılığının artması ve kullanıcıların öğrenme eğrisinin kısalması.
Süreç Yönetimi
Tasarım Handoff sürecinin netleşmesi.
Final Tasarım ve kod çıktılarının eşleşmesi

*Tasarım borcu (Design debt), bir ürünün veya sistemin tasarımı yapılırken, hızlı bir çözüm bulmak veya kısa vadeli teslim tarihlerini yakalamak amacıyla ideal veya en iyi çözümden bilerek veya bilmeyerek sapılması durumunda ortaya çıkan, gelecekte ödenmesi gereken ek çalışma ve maliyettir.
5. İlk 90 Gün Aksiyon Planı
0–30 Gün: Temel Oluşturma (Foundation)
Kurumsal ERP tasarımı için modern temel prensipler dökümanı oluşturmak (Agito Tasarım Framework v1).
Devam eden projelere aktif katılım, iyileştirme ve kritik karar noktalarında UX yönlendirmesi.
Figma klasör, dosya düzeni ve Naming standardı yenilemesi.
Design Pattern Library temel seti ve Design Review Checklist'in ilk taslağı.
10–12 global benchmark incelemesi.
30–60 Gün: Standardizasyon (Alignment)
Agito Tasarım Framework v1 çıktılarının sunulması, onaylanması ve projelerde uygulanmaya başlanması.
Pattern Library v1’in (Reusable Design Pattern setleri) tamamlanması.
Modüller arası davranış eşleşmelerinin yapılması.
Veri girişinde hiyerarşi prensiplerinin oturtulması.
60–90 Gün: Yaygınlaştırma ve Governance (Scaling)
Tasarım dosyalarının “single source of truth” haline getirilmesi.
Tasarım kalite kontrol süreçlerinin resmileştirilmesi.

Agito – Tasarım Planı
